# REA

Use REA when a claim depends on a shipped binary or package, decompilation,
passive application runtime evidence, or comparison with
behavior not established by available source. For ordinary analysis of a
complete source repository, use normal repository tools and do not run REA
readiness or provider commands.

## Connect only when needed

Installing this skill supplies instructions; it does not register the REA MCP
server, install analysis engines, or add tools to an already-running agent.

If REA tools are available and their registration is not known to be stale,
proceed directly to the target. Do not run diagnostics or setup before every
investigation. Use the connected server's actual tool list and input schemas;
a skill installed from repository main may describe capabilities absent from
an older npm release. Keep complete inline Evidence when that server does not
advertise retained references.

If tools are absent, a call is blocked by the client, or a required provider is
unavailable, read [connection and recovery](references/connection-and-recovery.md).
Diagnose the observed blocker, continue useful supported work, and resume the
original task after repair. Client approval, shell PATH, registration and engine
availability have different remedies; avoid repeating setup for all of them.

Prefer an existing `rea` CLI. When none is installed and REA package acquisition
is within the authorized scope, use `npx -y rea-agents@6.4.0` in its place.
Configuration repair starts with a read-only setup plan; show paths, backups and
skill replacement before applying authorized changes. Preserve existing approval
and target-execution boundaries. REA setup never installs or upgrades unrelated
software, including Node.js, npm, Homebrew, Java, Ghidra, IDA, JADX, Binwalk or Unblob.

## Route the target first

Choose the first tool from the target the user supplied. Use `open_binary` for
active-target native or archive workflows; target-free tools take their own
explicit path or endpoint and do not need it.

- ASAR or extracted JavaScript/Electron tree:
  `analyze_javascript_application`.
- Archive/package member inventory (ZIP/APK/IPA/MSIX/AppX or DMG):
  `open_binary` with the supplied local path; use `inspect_artifact` when its
  graph and findings help answer the question.
- Android APK code, classes, methods, or incoming references:
  `inspect_android_package`, then focused Android tools when advertised.
  Archive member inventory still uses the archive route above.
- Managed PE/CLI assembly: `inspect_managed_artifact`.
- Firmware image: `inspect_firmware_regions` when advertised. Use
  `extract_firmware` when extraction is requested, with a caller-selected new
  absolute output directory. These tools use caller-supplied Binwalk/Unblob on
  Linux; see the [firmware guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/firmware-analysis.md).
- .NET NativeAOT PE/ELF: native Ghidra analysis. Native code can yield pseudocode;
  consult the NativeAOT workflow in `references/native-and-artifacts.md` for
  optional metadata recovery and its supported host/layout boundary.
- User-owned browser page already open: `list_browser_targets`.
- Retained HAR/native mitmproxy capture: `inspect_web_network_capture`. Select
  original record ordinals when useful; inspect producer fields and byte/number
  sidecars without fetching recorded URLs or claiming live attribution. See
  [historical captures](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/web-network-captures.md) for the exact upstream
  profile and credential exclusions.
- User-owned Electron runtime already open: `list_electron_targets`.
- Explicit local EVM bytecode carrier: `inspect_evm_interface` with caller-selected
  `raw` or `hex` encoding. Preserve carrier/decoded digests and treat selectors,
  argument strings and mutability as inferred candidates; this performs no chain
  lookup or target execution. See the
  [EVM bytecode guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/evm-bytecode.md).
- Explicit Linux ELF file for offline layout, symbols, relocations or static
  mitigation evidence: `inspect_binary_layout`. This target-free operation uses
  caller-supplied pwntools without opening a disassembler database. Preserve its
  raw locations and inference/coverage limits; see the
  [offline binary guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/binary-diagnostics.md).
- Supplied Linux x86-64 ELF core: `inspect_recorded_crash`. Read every recorded
  thread, raw note and source-bound register without opening a live target.
  Historical PIDs do not select live processes. Request `include_debugger_context`
  only when core-only mapping candidates help; see the
  [recorded crash guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/recorded-crashes.md).
- PE resource type/name/language trees and static icon group references:
  `inspect_pe_resources` with an explicit path. This portable operation requires
  no active target or engine; opaque payloads retain file ranges and SHA-256.
- Native executable, library, or analysis database: `open_binary`, then
  use focused analysis tools directly; call `binary_overview` when metadata or
  inventory context is useful and available from the selected provider.

For an existing IDA MCP configuration, use `open_binary` with the original
input binary and `provider_id: "ida"`; do not pass an `.idb` or `.i64` database.
The legacy attached profile binds the already-open GUI input by SHA-256 and
leaves its database open. The modern headless profile creates and closes an
owned private database without saving. Installation and registration reuse
[mrexodia/ida-pro-mcp](https://github.com/mrexodia/ida-pro-mcp); see the
[IDA provider guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/ida-provider.md).
Proceed directly to `analyze_function`, pseudocode, or function/string searches
after opening; IDA does not supply `binary_overview`. Consult session availability
when composing broader workflows. Modern direct callers and unsupported dossier
facets remain unknown; live IDA results are not replayed from snapshots.

If the user says only "my app" without a name, path, or target already selected
for this task, ask which app before searching for artifacts. Paths in unrelated
saved reports and nearby test fixtures do not select the user's target. Resolve
an actual human-readable app name to one clear installed artifact when possible;
ask when matches are ambiguous. Never choose an example app on the user's behalf.

In a target-free session, use `open_binary` to bind any archive/package or
native target whose analysis tool operates on the active target. Do not call an
unadvertised tool; inspect `binary_session` with `{}` and its
`result.tool_availability` for availability reasons and remediation. The tool
list stays complete; availability depends on the operation, target, and host.

## Work summary-first

Start with the default result and use its inline Evidence and graph context.
For a JavaScript application or ELF layout whose size is unknown or large,
call `analyze_javascript_application` or `inspect_binary_layout` with
`"detail": "summary"`, then inspect the retained `parent_evidence_id` with
`inspect_analysis_view`.
Page module identities and select the returned `node_id` before requesting an
item; a single file path can represent several graph roles. Do not repeat an
identical analysis or rejected call without a changed input or resolved blocker.
Recheck session state when a lifecycle change makes that status useful.
Make a focused follow-up only when the
returned result leaves a specific question unanswered.

If MCP reports `resource_constraint` with `details.resource: "transport"`, call
`inspect_analysis_view` with its reported same-session Evidence reference for a
summary, one section/module, or a stable page. Use `trace_application_feature`
once a seed is known, or call `export_evidence_bundle` to write the complete
session to a selected path. The analysis remains complete in the ledger; avoid
repeating it merely to request the same oversized response. Complete CLI JSON
output also streams without a single MCP frame.

When the retained parent ID is unknown, use `get_evidence_bundle` with
`detail: "summary"` and exact filters for operation, target SHA-256,
analysis-profile digest or canonical procedure address. Select its returned
Evidence ID for the next view instead of requesting the complete bundle.
`retention: "complete-record"` means the whole record is stored; native
value-flow truncation and unknown metadata remain separate. For an exported
portable bundle, use `rea inspect-evidence-bundle` with JSON containing `path`,
`detail: "summary"` and the same `filters`. Reading that file still requires
validating its full contents within the local memory budget.

A client can also truncate a successful tool result without a REA transport
error. Treat that preview as incomplete; use the retained parent ID for smaller
views or export when the full record is needed. Do not parse the preview as
canonical JSON or repeat the producer. For JavaScript, read the
[module-view and trace recipes](references/javascript-applications.md) before
drilling into a large graph; a broad bidirectional trace can expand unrelated
containment relationships even after summary analysis.

Every conclusion must distinguish observations, inferences, and unknowns. Cite
Evidence IDs, preserve limitations and incomplete coverage, and never imply
that static analysis observed execution. Runtime requests execute the declared
target and lifecycle; do not broaden the target or action beyond those fields.

Within the user's requested investigation, call available analysis tools
directly. REA does not require permission grants or per-call approval flags.
The agent client can independently require approval. Follow the declared request
scope and the host's actual access requirements; use the recovery guide for an
observed client-policy rejection.

## Plan broader investigations

For requests that span multiple features or subsystems, use a staged workflow:

1. Turn the request into a checklist of questions and the evidence each answer
   needs. Resolve target identity and constraints from the conversation and
   workspace before asking for information again.
2. Inspect the current REA session, artifact identity, saved analysis database,
   bookmarks, and prior evidence. Reuse matching state; do not open duplicate
   sessions or repeat identical analysis.
3. Start with the smallest useful overview or inventory. Follow each question
   from its entry point through relevant data and state changes to its result.
   Batch related operations around a specific hypothesis, then expand only when
   the returned evidence leaves a concrete gap.
4. Inspect relevant packaged resources and configuration alongside code when
   they affect the question. Use format-aware inventory and parsers; do not
   infer behavior from filenames, strings, or layout alone.
5. Corroborate a conclusion with the evidence type it requires. Use runtime
   observation only when static evidence cannot answer the question and the
   required host runtime and OS access are available. For JavaScript behavior,
   run probes against the actual app through the available browser, Electron,
   or process capture workflow.
6. Decompose work into independent questions. When parallel workers are
   available and the questions do not depend on one another, assign distinct
   scopes, point workers to existing evidence, and ask them to return sources,
   conclusions, and unresolved gaps. Otherwise, work sequentially.
7. Keep a concise finding ledger linking each conclusion to Evidence IDs,
   confidence/evidence type, search boundary, and remaining unknowns. Update
   the shared index or investigation report so later passes can reuse results.

Before finishing, revisit the original checklist. Mark each question as
answered, partially answered, or unresolved based on its evidence; keep
bounded negative searches bounded, and do not describe a broad investigation
as complete while required questions remain open.

## Read only the relevant guide

- Native binaries, managed assemblies, archives, and extraction:
  [references/native-and-artifacts.md](references/native-and-artifacts.md)
- ASARs, extracted JavaScript, feature tracing, and version comparison:
  [references/javascript-applications.md](references/javascript-applications.md)
- Android APK declarations, classes, methods, and static references:
  [references/android-applications.md](references/android-applications.md)
- Passive browser/Electron observation and static/runtime reconciliation:
  [references/runtime-observation.md](references/runtime-observation.md)
- Evidence paging, comparisons, residual unknowns, and verification:
  [references/evidence-workflows.md](references/evidence-workflows.md)

## Finish the task

Explain findings in plain language and tie them to returned evidence. When the
user asks to build something, use normal coding tools and separate observed
behavior from design choices. Close an opened native session with
`close_binary` when the investigation is complete.

## assistant

I got a read on Spreeder, but **not through REA.** REA's browser capture failed with "Browser scenario provider is closing" and returned no data. Instead I used a tab in your signed-in Chrome. I read the live interface and searched the app's main code file for route and feature names. Screenshots kept timing out, so I never saw the reader running. Anything below drawn from names in the code is an inference about what exists, not a feature I tested.

## What it is

Spreeder is a speed-reading web app made by eReflect. It's built in Angular and served from `resources.ereflect.com`. The basic idea: you add text, and the app shows it to you a word or a small group of words at a time at a speed you set. Then it gives you practice tools, quizzes and courses to raise that speed over time.

## How you use it (checked in the live app)

- **Sidebar:** Add Text, Spreed, Tags, Courses, Profile.
- **Library:** a "Recent" shelf. Yours only holds the sample book, "Getting Started with Spreeder." The search bar says it's searching 20,504 books, so there's a built-in catalogue.
- **Add Text** gives four ways to bring in material:
  1. Paste Text
  2. Import Document
  3. AI Book Summary
  4. AI Generate (both AI options use "AI credits")
- **Tags** sort your library into groups (`library/tags/:name`).

## The reader (inferred from code names)

- **Speed and chunks:** you set words per minute (WPM) and a chunk size. The code also has settings for chunk delays and for pausing at punctuation.
- **Display:** font, size, colour, weight, line spacing, background colour and background graphics, plus a highlight colour.
- **Context view and pacer:** there's a context mode that shows the surrounding text, a pacer, and highlighting that moves through the text.
- **Countdown, pause and replay controls.**
- **Text-to-speech:** a choice of voices and speeds, using the browser's built-in speech.
- **Normal reading:** an e-reader mode and an immersive reader, for reading without flashing words.
- **Stats:** WPM, total words, progress and streaks.

## Training (inferred from code names)

- **Courses:** step-by-step lessons grouped into curricula, with replay and preview (`courses/:curriculum/:course`).
- **Speed-reading games:** Speed Builder, Focus, Catch, Falling, Rising and Sinking Words, Letter Train I and II, Key Memory, Key React, and Quick Trainer.
- **Word work:** word lists with learn, review, edit and "word scroll" modes, plus flashcards and quizzes.
- **Classroom features:** admin pages, invites, Nearpod activity links and a "Typesy" import. Typesy is eReflect's typing product, so this edition is clearly built to sell to schools too.

## Why it might matter for LSAT U

The useful parts for you are the chunked display with WPM control and the context view, plus the quizzes that follow reading. That's close to a timed RC passage reader with a comprehension check, which LSAT U could build itself.

Your Chrome now has a tab I opened on the `/spreed` page. You can close it whenever you like.

 turnover among workers in fast-food franchises. In fact, these businesses have the highest turnover rate of any industry in the U.S. The entire workforce of the fast-food industry turns over three times in a year. Although the simple, repetitive nature of the work makes it easy to replace those who leave, the organization would clearly benefit from keeping employees longer. The costs of hiring and training are magnified when the turnover rate is extraordinarily high. The application of the rational model to the house-building process in the 1950s and ’60s led to suburban communities consisting of nearly identical structures. Indeed, it was possible to wander into the residence of someone else and not to realize immediately that one was not at home. The more expensive developments were superficially more diversified, but their interior layouts assumed residents who were indistinguishable in their requirements. Tool result:
 in their requirements. Furthermore, the planned communities themselves look very similar. Established trees are bulldozed to facilitate construction. In their place, a number of saplings, held up by posts and wire, are planted. Streets are laid out in symmetrical grid patterns. With such uniformity, suburbanites may well enter the wrong subdivision or become lost in their own. Many of Steven Spielberg’s films are set in such suburbs. Spielberg’s strategy is to lure the viewer into this highly repetitive world and then to have a completely unexpected event occur. For example, the film Poltergeist takes place in a conventional suburban household in which evil spirits ultimately disrupt the sameness. (The spirits first manifest themselves through another key element of the homogeneous society—the television set.) ## assistant

I found your passage and went through the Spreeder reader setting by setting. I haven't built anything yet. Before I do, I need to know where it should go.

## Your passage

"Living in a rational society" is in your library under "User added." It's about 550 words on how society keeps pursuing efficiency, using fast food, cookie-cutter suburbs and Spielberg films as examples. At Spreeder's default 200 WPM it's about a 3-minute read. I pulled the full text out of the app, so I can load it as the first preset passage.

## Everything the reader does (checked live)

**Five reading modes, each with its own settings:**

| Mode | What it does | Settings |
|---|---|---|
| Serial | Flashes words one chunk at a time in the same spot | Number of lines, words per line, centre text |
| Flow | Text moves through in a line | Words per line |
| Highlight | The whole passage stays on screen; the current word lights up and the rest is dimmed | Words per line, number of lines |
| Scroll | The text scrolls past (couldn't load its settings; see below) | not confirmed |
| e-Reader | Ordinary page reading | not confirmed |

**Advanced settings:**
- **Punctuation pauses:** a slight pause at the end of sentences and at punctuation. It only works when one word shows at a time.
- **Speed variability:** an on/off switch. It has no explanation in the app; I'd guess it varies timing by word length.
- **Restore defaults.**

**The controls around the reader:**
- **Speed:** WPM set with −100, −10, +10 and +100 buttons, then Save.
- **Play/pause, plus back and forward** one word.
- **Progress bar:** "1% – 3 minutes remaining."
- **View:** the full text in a panel with a search box. The word you're on is boxed, and you can jump to any word from there.
- **Fullscreen, bookmark, and a keyboard-shortcuts button.**
- **Per-user settings:** the panel is titled "mk's preferences" and the choices persist.

**Library:**
- Add Text has four options: paste text, import a document, AI book summary and AI generate.
- Your texts are grouped into Recent and Added By Me.
- Tags, and a search across 20,504 built-in books.

## What I couldn't check

- **Appearance tab:** it opened blank both times I tried. It's probably where font, colours and background live (those settings exist in the code), but I couldn't see them.
- **Scroll and e-Reader settings:** switching to Scroll threw a "no internet connection" error, and I didn't get to e-Reader's panel.
- **Courses:** the page says "No curriculums or courses assigned to your class yet."
- **Games:** the speed games (Speed Builder, Falling Words and the rest) are named in the code, but I found no menu for them in your account. They're probably part of a different plan or the classroom version.
- **Quizzes and flashcards:** both are named in the code, but I saw no way to open them in your account.
- **The keyboard-shortcuts button and the passage's ⋮ menu:** not opened.

**One thing I changed in your account:** your reading mode is now Scroll instead of Serial. WPM is still 200. Set it back in the gear menu, or tell me to.

## One question before I build

Should this go into **LSAT U** as a new reading tool, with a fixed list of passages you choose (starting with this one) and every reader feature above? Or do you want it somewhere else, like a standalone page?

Reader (`/spreed`)\n\n### Chrome around the text\n- Top: Back, title, **Search** (e-Reader only), **Fullscreen**, **Bookmarks**, **Settings (gear)**.\n- Bottom: **Keyboard shortcuts** button, **WPM button** (shows current speed), **View**, progress (\"1% - 3 minutes remaining\").\n- Transport: previous chunk ◁, play/pause, next chunk ▷.\n\n### Speed (WPM) dialog\n- \"Reading Speed (WPM)\" with **−100, −10, +10, +100** buttons, Cancel / Save. Default 200.\n\n### View panel\n- Full passage overlay with \"User added\" badge, **Search this text**, current word boxed, click any word to jump,\n  play button, footer \"3 minutes to go - 200 WPM\".\n\n### Bookmarks\n- Empty state: \"To create a bookmark, simply pause Spreeder while you're reading and long press or click on any word.\"\n\n### Keyboard shortcuts\n| Keys | Action |\n|---|---|\n| ↑ / ↓ or + / − | increase / decrease speed |\n| P or Space | pause / resume |\n| → | section forward |\n| ← | section back |\n| R | restart |\n| S | settings |\n\n## 4. Preferences dialog (\"<name>'s preferences\")\n\nTabs: **Spreed · Appearance · Advanced**, plus **Restore Defaults**. Saved per user, server-side.\n\n### Spreed tab: Reading Mode and its per-mode settings\n| Mode | What it looks like | Settings (ranges) |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Serial** | One chunk flashed in a fixed spot | Number of Lines (1–6), Words Per Line (1–6), Center Text (toggle, default on) |\n| **Flow** | Chunk flashes, positioned where it sits in the line | Words Per Line (1–6) |\n| **Highlight** | Whole text visible, current chunk bright, rest dimmed | Words Per Line (1–6), Number of Lines (1–6) |\n| **Scroll** | Text scrolls past | Words Per Line (1–6) |\n| **e-Reader** | Normal paginated reading (\"1 / 6\"), page slider, prev/next page, in-text search | Theme, Font, Size, Line Spacing (1.0–2.0, step 0.1) |\n\n### Appearance tab (all modes)\n- **Theme:** Light, Dark, High Contrast, System.\n- **Font (19):** Arial, Times New Roman, Verdana, Calibri, Microsoft Sans Serif, Impact, Lucida Console,\n  Comic Sans MS, Georgia, Palatino Linotype, Tahoma, Trebuchet MS, Courier New, Open Dyslexic Font, Antikor,\n  Code New Roman, CQ Mono, Pointfree, Fira Mono.\n- **Size:** 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 22, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 50, 56, 62, 68, 74 (default 36).\n- Live \"Sample Text\" preview.\n- Note: in speed modes this tab renders blank (Spreeder bug); the controls exist in the DOM and show\n  correctly in e-Reader mode and under Profile → Settings.\n\n### Advanced tab\n- **Punctuation Pauses** (default on): \"Slight pause at end of sentences and punctuation marks. Only works for group size of 1.\"\n- **Speed Variability** (default off): no help text shown. *Inference:* varies display time per chunk (e.g. by word length).\n\n## 5. Profile (`/profile`)\n\n- Header: name, AI credit balance, share button.\n- **Dashboard:** Vital Stats gauges (Current Speed, Target Speed, 0–2000 scale), Reading Focus, Training Time,\n  Reading Speed charts, each with refresh and a period picker.\n- **Stats:** metric picker (Reading Speed, Reading Focus, Training Time) × period (24 Hours, 7 Days, 14 Days, 30 Days, 6 Months).\n- **Options:** Theme (submenu), Edit Profile, **Goals** (Reading Speed Goal slider 50–5000 WPM, default 600,\n  with Recommend button; copy: \"An average person reads at 200 words per minute. Not inspected\n\n- Fullscreen (standard browser fullscreen assumed), Edit Profile form, Import Document accepted file types,\n  AI Book Summary / AI Generate dialogs (both need credits; account has 0), Tags page contents, eReflect app grid.\n\n## 9. First predetermined passage\n\n**Living in a rational society** (user added, ~550 words, ~3 min at 200 WPM)\n\nThe rationalizing of society can be conceptualized as the pursuit of efficiency, predictability, calculability, and control through technology. But rational systems inevitably spawn a series of irrationalities that result in the compromising and perhaps even the undermining of their rationality. Fast-food restaurants, which epitomize the rational model, proffer the fastest means of getting from a hungry state to a sated one, without surprises, at low cost, in a carnival-like setting suggesting that fun awaits the consumer at each visit. The wholesomeness of the food seems an insignificant consideration. Whereas in the past, working people were prepared to spend up to an hour preparing dinner, they now are impatient if a meal is not on the table within ten minutes. (For their part, some fast-food restaurants have developed chairs that become uncomfortable after about twenty minutes, to ensure that diners do not stay long.) Fast-food restaurants have preferentially recruited adolescent help, at least until recently, because this age group adjusts more easily than adults do to surrendering their autonomy to machines, rules, and procedures. Few skills are required on the job, so workers are asked to use only a minute portion of their abilities. This policy is irrational from the standpoint of the organization, since it could obtain much more from its employees for the money (however negligible) it pays them. These minimal skill demands are also irrational from the perspective of the employees, who are not allowed to think or to respond creatively to the demands of the work. These restrictions lead to high levels of resentment, job dissatisfaction, alienation, absenteeism, and turnover among workers in fast-food franchises. In fact, these businesses have the highest turnover rate of any industry in the U.S. The entire workforce of the fast-food industry turns over three times in a year. Although the simple, repetitive nature of the work makes it easy to replace those who leave, the organization would clearly benefit from keeping employees longer. The costs of hiring and training are magnified when the turnover rate is extraordinarily high.\n\nThe application of the rational model to the house-building process in the 1950s and ’60s led to suburban communities consisting of nearly identical structures. Indeed, it was possible to wander into the residence of someone else and not to realize immediately that one was not at home. The more expensive developments were superficially more diversified, but their interior layouts assumed residents who were indistinguishable in their requirements. Furthermore, the planned communities themselves look very similar. Established trees are bulldozed to facilitate construction. In their place, a number of saplings, held up by posts and wire, are planted. Streets are laid out in symmetrical grid patterns. With such uniformity, suburbanites may well enter the wrong subdivision or become lost in their own.\n\nMany of Steven Spielberg’s films are set in such suburbs. Spielberg’s strategy is to lure the viewer into this highly repetitive world and then to have a completely unexpected event occur. For example, the film Poltergeist takes place in a conventional suburban household in which evil spirits ultimately disrupt the sameness. (The spirits first manifest themselves through another key element of the homogeneous society—the television set.) The great success of Spielberg’s films may be traceable to a longing for some unpredictability, even if it is bizarre and menacing, in increasingly routinized lives.\n\nParagraph breaks are inferred; Spreeder's word view does not preserve them.\n"}

I've gone through every Spreeder screen your account can reach and written it all into one spec: [research/2026-10-12-spreeder-feature-inventory.md](research/2026-10-12-spreeder-feature-inventory.md). It has every setting and its exact options and ranges, plus your passage, so we can build the replica from it.

**The reader has five modes, each with its own settings:**
- **Serial:** words flash one chunk at a time in the same spot. Lines 1–6, words per line 1–6, and a centre-text switch.
- **Flow:** the chunk flashes where it sits in the line. Words per line 1–6.
- **Highlight:** the whole passage stays on screen and the current chunk lights up. Words per line 1–6, lines 1–6.
- **Scroll:** the text scrolls past. Words per line 1–6.
- **e-Reader:** normal reading in pages (your passage is 6 pages), with a page slider and search. You can also set line spacing from 1.0 to 2.0.

**Settings that apply in every mode:**
- **Look:** 4 themes (Light, Dark, High Contrast, System), 19 fonts including OpenDyslexic, and 21 text sizes from 8 to 74.
- **Pauses:** a slight pause at punctuation, which only works when one word shows at a time.
- **Speed variability:** an on/off switch the app doesn't explain.
- **Restore defaults.**

**Around the reader:**
- **Speed:** WPM set with −100, −10, +10 and +100 buttons.
- **View:** the full passage with a search box; click any word to jump there.
- **Bookmarks:** pause, then click or long-press a word.
- **Keyboard shortcuts:** ↑/↓ or +/− change speed, P or Space pauses, ← and → move back and forward a section, R restarts, S opens settings.
- **Progress:** percentage done and minutes left.

**Library and profile:**
- **Passage menu:** View/Location, Edit Image, Edit Text, Tag, Delete, and Hide from Recent.
- **Add Text:** paste with a title, import a document, or the two AI options.
- **Profile:** speed gauges, and charts for reading speed, focus and training time over 24 hours to 6 months. Also a goal setting from 50 to 5,000 WPM (default 600).

**What's still missing:**
- **Games, quizzes, flashcards, word lists and read-aloud:** these exist in Spreeder's code, but your account has no screen for any of them, so I can't see how they behave. They're marked in the spec as unverified.

Data model (Supabase / Postgres)\n\n```sql\ncreate table speed_reader_passages (\n  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),\n  slug text unique not null,\n  title text not null,\n  author text,\n  body text not null,             -- plain text; paragraphs separated by a blank line\n  cover_url text,                 -- optional; fall back to a generic document icon\n  word_count int not null,        -- computed on write\n  sort_order int not null default 0,\n  is_published boolean not null default true,\n  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),\n  updated_at timestamptz not null default now()\n);\n\ncreate table speed_reader_preferences (\n  user_id uuid primary key references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,\n  wpm int not null default 200 check (wpm between 50 and 5000),\n  reading_mode text not null default 'serial'\n    check (reading_mode in ('serial','flow','highlight','scroll','ereader')),\n  number_of_lines int not null default 1 check (number_of_lines between 1 and 6),\n  words_per_line int not null default 1 check (words_per_line between 1 and 6),\n  center_text boolean not null default true,\n  theme text not null default 'system' check (theme in ('light','dark','high_contrast','system')),\n  font_family text not null default 'Arial',\n  font_size int not null default 36,\n  line_spacing numeric(2,1) not null default 1.0 check (line_spacing between 1.0 and 2.0),\n  punctuation_pauses boolean not null default true,\n  speed_variability boolean not null default false,\n  goal_wpm int not null default 600 check (goal_wpm between 50 and 5000),\n  updated_at timestamptz not null default now()\n);\n\ncreate table speed_reader_progress (\n  user_id uuid references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,\n  passage_id uuid references speed_reader_passages(id) on delete cascade,\n  word_index int not null default 0,\n  completed_at timestamptz,\n  last_opened_at timestamptz not null default now(),\n  hidden_from_recent boolean not null default false,\n  primary key (user_id, passage_id)\n);\n\ncreate table speed_reader_bookmarks (\n  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),\n  user_id uuid references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,\n  passage_id uuid references speed_reader_passages(id) on delete cascade,\n  word_index int not null,\n  created_at timestamptz not null default now()\n);\n\ncreate table speed_reader_sessions (\n  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),\n  user_id uuid references auth.users(id) on delete cascade,\n  passage_id uuid references speed_reader_passages(id) on delete cascade,\n  reading_mode text not null,\n  wpm int not null,                 -- setting at the time\n  words_read int not null,\n  active_ms int not null,           -- time actually playing, pauses excluded\n  started_at timestamptz not null,\n  ended_at timestamptz not null\n);\n```\n\nRLS: passages readable by any authenticated user where `is_published`; writable by admins only.\nAll other tables: a user reads and writes only their own rows; admins read all.\n\nSeed migration: insert passage 1 (section 11).\n\n## 5. Text processing (shared by every mode)\n\nWrite one pure, unit-tested module `speedReader/tokenize.ts`:\n\n- Split `body` into paragraphs on blank lines, paragraphs into words on whitespace. Keep punctuation\n  attached to its word. Each token: `{ index, text, paragraphIndex, endsSentence, hasPunctuation }`.\n  - `endsSentence`: word ends with `.`, `!`, `?` (allowing a trailing `)` `\"` `’`).\n  - `hasPunctuation`: word ends with `,` `;` `:` `—` `)`.\n- `chunk(tokens, wordsPerLine, numberOfLines)` groups tokens into display chunks. Chunk size =\n  `wordsPerLine × numberOfLines` words, laid out as `numberOfLines` rows of `wordsPerLine` words.\n  Chunks never cross a paragraph boundary.\n- Position everywhere is a **word index**, never a chunk index, so changing chunk size mid-read\n  keeps the reader's place (snap to the chunk containing that word).\n\n## 6. Timing engine\n\nOne hook, `useSpeedReaderClock`, drives every timed mode. Do not depend on a context object that is\nrecreated every tick; keep mutable state in refs.\n\n- Base time per word = `60000 / wpm` ms. Chunk duration = base × words in chunk.\n- **Punctuation pauses** (only when chunk size is exactly 1 word, matching Spreeder):\n  sentence end ×2.0, other punctuation ×1.5, paragraph end ×2.5. Make the multipliers constants.\n- **Speed variability** (Spreeder gives no description; this is our definition, keep it isolated so it\n  can be changed): scale each chunk by its length relative to the passage average,\n  `factor = clamp(0.75, 1.5, avgCharsPerWord ? chunkChars / (words × avgCharsPerWord) : 1)`, then\n  renormalise so total passage time still equals `wordCount × 60000 / wpm`.\n- Use `requestAnimationFrame` with elapsed-time accounting so tab throttling does not drift the speed.\n- Time remaining = remaining words × base time (with pauses), rounded up to whole minutes:\n  \"N minutes remaining\" / \"Less than a minute remaining\".\n- Record a `speed_reader_sessions` row on pause (if ≥ 5 s active), on completion and on exit.\n\n## 7. Reader screen (`/speed-reader/:slug`)\n\n### Layout\n- Top bar: Back (to library), passage title (centred), and on the right: Search (e-Reader only),\n  Fullscreen, Bookmarks, Settings.\n- Stage (centre): mode renderer.\n- Transport (timed modes): ◁ previous chunk, ▶/❚❚ play/pause (large circle), ▷ next chunk.\n- Bottom bar: keyboard-shortcuts button (left), WPM button showing current WPM, View button (centre),\n  progress text \"P% - N minutes remaining\" (right).\n- Black background by default (follows theme).\n\n### The five modes\n| Mode | Render | Settings shown on Spreed tab |\n|---|---|---|\n| Serial | Current chunk only, in a fixed box. Centred if Center Text on, else left-aligned. | Number of Lines, Words Per Line, Center Text |\n| Flow | Current chunk only, placed at the horizontal position it would occupy in the flowing line, so the eye travels left to right then returns. | Words Per Line |\n| Highlight | Whole passage visible, laid out `wordsPerLine` words per row. Current chunk full brightness, all else ~35% opacity. Auto-scroll keeps the current chunk vertically centred. | Words Per Line, Number of Lines |\n| Scroll | Passage laid out `wordsPerLine` words per row and scrolled upward continuously at the WPM rate through a fixed reading line; the word at the line is emphasised. | Words Per Line |\n| e-Reader | Normal paginated text sized to the viewport. No timer. Page indicator \"n / total\", page slider, prev/next page arrows, keyboard ← →. Search icon in the top bar searches the passage and jumps to the page of the hit. | Theme, Font, Size, Line Spacing (replaces the Spreed/Appearance split in this mode) |\n\nSwitching mode keeps the current word index.\n\n### Controls\n- **WPM dialog** \"Reading Speed (WPM)\": current value large, buttons −10, −100 (left) and +10, +100 (right),\n  Cancel / Save. Clamp 50–5000. Saving persists to preferences.\n- **View panel**: full-screen overlay. Title, small \"Passage\" badge, \"Search this text\" input that\n  highlights matches, full text with current word outlined, click any word to set position, play button,\n  footer \"N minutes to go - W WPM\". Close with ✕ or Esc.\n- **Bookmarks**: while paused, click or long-press a word (in Highlight, Scroll, View panel or e-Reader;\n  in Serial/Flow, the bookmark button on the paused stage bookmarks the current word) to add a bookmark.\n  Bookmarks dialog lists them as a text snippet around the word with date; click to jump, trash to delete.\n  Empty state: \"No bookmarks yet. To add one, pause and click any word.\"\n- **Fullscreen**: browser Fullscreen API toggle; icon swaps.\n- **Keyboard shortcuts** (popover from the keyboard button, and active globally on this page):\n\n| Keys | Action |\n|---|---|\n| ↑ or + | speed +10 WPM |\n| ↓ or − | speed −10 WPM |\n| P or Space | pause / resume |\n| → | next chunk (e-Reader: next page) |\n| ← | previous chunk (e-Reader: previous page) |\n| R | restart from the beginning |\n| S | open settings |\n\nIgnore shortcuts while focus is in a text input.\n\n- On reaching the end: stop, mark `completed_at`, show a completion card with words read, active time,\n  effective WPM, and buttons Read again / Back to library.\n- Resume: opening a passage starts at the saved `word_index` (with a one-time \"Resume from where you\n  left off? / Start over\" prompt if index > 0).\n\n## 8. Preferences dialog\n\nTitle \"Preferences\". Tabs **Spreed · Appearance · Advanced**. Footer button **Restore Defaults**\n(restores the defaults in section 4 after a confirm). Changes apply live and save on change (debounced).\n\n- **Spreed**: Reading Mode select (Serial, Flow, Highlight, Scroll, e-Reader), then the per-mode controls\n  from the table above. Each option rendered in its own face. Self-host the\n    non-system fonts (OpenDyslexic, Fira Mono, etc.) with a fallback stack; if a licence is unclear, ship\n    it with a fallback and flag it in the PR.\n  - Size: 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 22, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 50, 56, 62, 68, 74.\n  - Live \"Sample Text\" preview.\n  - Must actually render in every mode (Spreeder's version is blank in speed modes; do not copy that bug).\n- **Advanced**: Punctuation Pauses toggle with help \"Slight pause at the end of sentences and punctuation\n  marks. Use the app's design tokens for\neverything except High Contrast.\n\n## 9. Library (`/speed-reader`)\n\n- Shelves: **Continue reading** (progress > 0 and not complete, newest first, excludes hidden), then\n  **All passages** (admin `sort_order`).\n- Card: cover (or generic document icon on a coloured tile), title, author, word count, estimated minutes\n  at the student's WPM, a progress bar if started, a check if completed.\n- Card ⋮ menu: Open, View text, Hide from Continue reading.\n- Search box filters by title and author.\n- Empty state if no passages are published.\n\n## 10. Stats and goal (`/speed-reader/stats`)\n\n- Header: student name, a share-free layout.\n- **Vital stats**: two gauges 0–2000 WPM, Current Speed (median effective WPM over the last 5 sessions)\n  and Target Speed (goal).\n- Charts (Recharts or the app's chart lib): Reading Speed (effective WPM per session), Reading Focus\n  (share of session time spent playing vs paused), Training Time (active minutes per day). **One assumption to check:** I aimed it at **LSAT U**, as a new Speed Reader section behind a switch that keeps it hidden from students until you turn it on. You never confirmed that. If you want a standalone app instead, tell me and I'll change the target section.

**What the build covers:**
- **Your passage list:** stored in the database. You add, edit, reorder, hide or delete passages from an admin page, with no code change needed. "Living in a rational society" is loaded as the first one.
- **The reader:** all five modes (Serial, Flow, Highlight, Scroll, e-Reader), each with Spreeder's exact settings and ranges.

- **Speed variability:** Spreeder never explains it, so I defined it: longer words get a little more time, shorter words a little less, at the same overall speed.

- Students pasting or importing their own text, since you want a fixed list. The database can still support it later.

- **Quizzes and flashcards:** marked as not a focus. The builder is told not to build them or design around them, and I removed the open question about comprehension quizzes.
- **Word lists:** not built yet, but set up for the next phase. Every word in a passage keeps a fixed position, so a saved word can link straight back to the exact spot in its article. The builder has to explain in the PR where word lists would fit in.

import { useEffect } from "react";
import { useQuery, useQueryClient } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useLocation, useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { authUrlWithReturnTo } from "@/lib/authRedirect";
import { CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS, withDerivedAccess } from "@/lib/adminUsers";

export interface UserPermissions {
  has_bootcamp_access: boolean;
  has_classroom_access: boolean;
  has_analytics_access: boolean;
  has_schedule_access: boolean;
  has_practice_access: boolean;
  has_drill_access: boolean;
  has_waj_access: boolean;
  has_journal_access: boolean;
  has_flagged_access: boolean;
  has_chat_access: boolean;
  has_export_access: boolean;
  has_ta_access: boolean;
  has_activity_access: boolean;
  is_admin: boolean;
  loading: boolean;
}

const ACCESS_FLAGS = [
  "has_bootcamp_access",
  "has_classroom_access",
  "has_analytics_access",
  "has_schedule_access",
  "has_practice_access",
  "has_drill_access",
  "has_waj_access",
  "has_journal_access",
  "has_flagged_access",
  "has_chat_access",
  "has_export_access",
  "has_ta_access",
  "has_activity_access",
] as const;

export type PermissionFlag = (typeof ACCESS_FLAGS)[number];

// Narrow session-death classifier. Only three conditions count:
//  1. supabase.auth.getSession() returns no session while context says user is set
//  2. Supabase query error with HTTP status 401
//  3. Error message mentioning JWT / token-expired / invalid_token explicitly
// Everything else (including RLS 403s and null profile rows) is legitimate
// permission-denied and must fall through to the normal LockedModule path.
function isSessionDeadError(err: unknown): boolean {
  if (!err || typeof err !== 'object') return false;
  const details = err as { status?: unknown; message?: unknown };
  if (details.status === 401) return true;
  const msg = String(details.message || "").toLowerCase();
  return (
    msg.includes("jwt") ||
    msg.includes("token is expired") ||
    msg.includes("token expired") ||
    msg.includes("invalid_token")
  );
}

type PermissionFlags = Omit<UserPermissions, "loading">;

const DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS: PermissionFlags = {
  has_bootcamp_access: false,
  has_classroom_access: false,
  has_analytics_access: false,
  has_schedule_access: false,
  has_practice_access: false,
  has_drill_access: false,
  has_waj_access: false,
  has_journal_access: false,
  has_flagged_access: false,
  has_chat_access: false,
  has_export_access: false,
  has_ta_access: false,
  has_activity_access: false,
  is_admin: false,

export function useUserPermissions(): UserPermissions {
  const { user, authReady, signOut } = useAuth();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const location = useLocation();
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const userId = user?.id;

  // The hook mounts once per ProtectedRoute, so the subscription is guarded by
  // a single shared channel per user: the first mount opens it, the last unmount
  // closes it. Without this, every gated page would hold its own socket.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!authReady || !userId) return;
    const entry = acquireProfileWatch(userId, (row) => {
      // Ignore self-caused / unrelated row updates (e.g. presence heartbeats
      // writing last_seen_at). Only an actual access-flag change needs a
      // refetch; otherwise every heartbeat would re-trigger the permissions
      // query, which used to feed a fetch -> write -> event -> fetch loop.
      if (row) {
        const cached = queryClient.getQueryData<PermissionFlags>(["user-permissions", userId]);
        if (cached) {
          const archivedNow = "archived_at" in row && !!row.archived_at;
          const changed = CONFIGURABLE_FLAG_KEYS.some(
            (flag) => flag in row && !!row[flag] !== !!cached[flag],
          );
          if (!changed && !archivedNow) return;
        }
      }
      void queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["user-permissions", userId] });
    });
    return () => releaseProfileWatch(userId, entry);
  }, [authReady, userId, queryClient]);

  const query = useQuery<PermissionFlags>({
    // Cached per user, shared across all ProtectedRoute / consumer mounts, so
    // navigating between gated pages reads cached permissions synchronously
    // instead of re-fetching (and re-showing the full-screen loading gate).
    queryKey: ["user-permissions", userId],
    enabled: authReady && !!user,
    // Realtime (below) is the primary path for a revoke to land; the short
    // stale window and the focus refetch are the fallback when the socket is
    // down, so "Revoke All Access" is never more than a tab switch away from
    // taking effect. Cached data keeps `loading` false, so neither flashes.
    staleTime: 5 * 60_000,
    gcTime: 30 * 60_000,
    refetchOnWindowFocus: true,
    // Retry transient failures a couple times, but never retry a dead session
    // (that path signs the user out).
    retry: (count, err) =>
      !(err instanceof SessionExpiredError) && !(err instanceof AccountArchivedError) && count < 2,
    queryFn: async () => {
      const userId = user!.id;

      // Pre-check: is the session still alive on the client?
      const { data: { session } } = await supabase.auth.getSession();
      if (!session) throw new SessionExpiredError();

      // Fetch access flags from profile
      const { data: profile, error: profileErr } = await supabase
        .from("profiles")
        .select([...ACCESS_FLAGS, "archived_at"].join(", "))
        .eq("class_id", userId)
        .maybeSingle();
      if (isSessionDeadError(profileErr)) throw new SessionExpiredError();
      // A non-session read failure (transient network / 5xx) must NOT be
      // silently converted to all-false permissions; throw so TanStack Query
      // records the error and retries instead of caching bogus denied flags.

22:- **`/pickup`** (start / "brief me"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.
23:- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / "done for today"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.
31:- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`
32:- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`
33:- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`
34:- A student's whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule
35:- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)
39:- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)
40:- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`
41:- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)
42:- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`
43:- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`
47:## Commands
51:- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080
52:- `npm run build` — production build
53:- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags
54:- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle
55:- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)
56:- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)
57:- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode
58:- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`
62:- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.
63:- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).
67:## Architecture (big picture)
69:- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.
70:- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.
71:- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.
72:- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.
73:- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.
74:- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.
75:- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.
76:- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.
77:- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.
78:- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.
80:## Conventions
82:- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.
83:- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.
84:- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable's "Revert to commit", "revert and resend", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental "revert and resend" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.
85:- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).
86:- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.
87:- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:
91:- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **"Joshua"**, students display their real profile names.
93:## Cross-tool instruction hygiene
97:- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.
98:- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.
99:- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.
